Madam,

In accordance with the provisions of Section 19(2) of the International Financial Services Centres
Authority Act, 2019, | forward herewith a copy of the Annual Report of the International Financial
Services Centres Authority (IFSCA) for the year ended March 31, 2026, in the format prescribed in
the International Financial Services Centres Authority (Annual Report and Returns and
Statements and Other Particulars) Rules, 2022, notified on February 28, 2022, in Part II—Section
3—Sub-section (i) of the Gazette of India Extraordinary.

Conventions used in this Report

%/ INR: Rupees (Rs.)                      hundred crore (1,000,000,000)
Lakh: One hundred thousand (100,000)      lakh crore (1,000,000,000,000)
Crore: One hundred lakh (10,000,000)
Million (Mn): Ten lakh (1,000,000)
Billion (Bn): One thousand million/ One
Trillion (Tn): One thousand billion/ One

The analyses, statistics, and insights presented in this Report are based on information sourced
from periodic regulatory filings, official submissions by Regulated Entities (REs), and other

credible data sources available with the Authority. Figures presented in tables, graphs and
charts may be subject to rounding off, and accordingly, the totals may not exactly match the

sum of the individual components. In the event of any discrepancy between the data or figures

presented in this Report and those published in earlier publications of the Authority, the data
and figures contained in this Report shall be deemed to be the latest revised and updated

information.

This Report can also be accessed on internet at: https://ifsca.gov.in/

GIFT IFSC was_~ established to meet
precisely these demands. The preceding
sections have set out the macroeconomic and
financial context within which GIFT IFSC
operated during FY 2025-26. The sections that
follow document the outcomes delivered by
GIFT IFSC: the growth in banking assets,
capital market activity, fund management,
insurance, bullion and FinTech that together
constitute the Authority’s operational record
for the year, and also the regulatory and
policy developments that will shape the
trajectory of the financial centre in the years
ahead.

CAPITAL MARKETS

Capital markets in the International Financial                  Stock Exchange

Services Centre (IFSC) operate under a

globally aligned framework governing                            Stock exchanges perform a central role in
                                                                facilitating the trading of securities in
issuance, listing, trading, clearing and
                                                                coordination with clearing corporations and
settlement of securities and financial                          depositories. They provide transparent and
                                                                efficient trading platforms that enable price
instruments, including equity shares, debt                      discovery for listed securities. In addition,
                                                                stock exchanges are entrusted with important
instruments, units of funds and derivatives.                    regulatory responsibilities aimed at ensuring
                                                                market integrity, orderly functioning of the
These activities support capital formation,
                                                                market, and protection of investor interests.
investment and risk management through

internationally  benchmarked                        market

infrastructure in GIFT IFSC.

During FY 2025-26, capital market activities in                 Recognition to stock exchanges is granted by

IFSC continued to expand, supported by                          the IFSCA under regulation 9 of the IFSCA

regulatory frameworks aligned with                              (Market                   Infrastructure  Institutions)

international best practices and a sustained                    Regulations, 2021 (MII Regulations).
focus on ease of doing business.

infrastructure institutions, including stock                    International Exchange (IFSC) Limited (India

exchanges, clearing corporations and                            INX) and NSE IFSC Limited (NSE IX). India INX
depositories, and a wide range of capital
market intermediaries, with the regulatory                      is a subsidiary of BSE Limited, while NSE IX is
framework subject to periodic review based

on market feedback.
                                                                Exchange of India Limited. Both the stock

IFSCA Bullion Good Delivery Guidelines

IFSCA is in the process of notifying guidelines
defining the standards and procedures for the
good delivery of bullion, including

specifications on weight, purity, and refiner
accreditation. These guidelines aim to create

uniformity and global acceptance of bullion
traded on the IFSC platform.

by the International Financial Services                  various domains to effectively regulate

AUDIT COMMITTEES

In accordance with Para 4 (2) of the
International Financial Services Centres
Authority (Form of Annual Statement of
Accounts) Rules, 2022, IFSCA Audit
Committee has been constituted with the

following members:

1. Shri Manoj Kumar, Member, IFSCA (Chairperson)
2. Ms. Aparna Sinha, Member, IFSCA (Member)
3. Shri K. Mahipal Reddy, ED, IFSCA (Member)

The Audit Committee approved the Audited
Annual Statement of Accounts for FY 2024-25,
on Dec 19, 2025, for the same to be tabled in
both the houses of the Parliament.
